# decisions — architectural decision record (ADR)

<!-- One line per decision. `affects:` is not optional: a decision nobody can
     trace to code is a decision nobody can revisit. check_blueprint.py and the
     postflight both read it.

     D-001: <decision> — <rationale> — affects: <system(s) / path(s)> — <date>
-->

D-001: Reconnect BoardDistributor to runtime for live/dynamic board food-spawn distribution (replacing DayContentGenerator's pre-authored BoardTimeline replay for everything except Day Start, TriggerStepIndex == -1, which stays authored), with guaranteed-ticket selection made probabilistic (a per-round budget, Poisson-sampled or manually fixed, per-Day configurable; picks tickets via an arrival-order-weighted lottery; any active ticket under a configurable remaining-time threshold, default 10s, is unconditionally guaranteed and consumes from — and may exceed — that budget) — rationale: ExpoTheExplorer/CLAUDE.md Section 3 ("Board / Food Distribution"), still marked Locked, already describes this live required-pool/noise-pool system as current design; it was never updated when an earlier, undocumented change (referred to only in code comments as "PR-6") disconnected BoardDistributor from runtime because a live ticket-lookahead buffer over-consumed the Day's fixed-length pre-authored TicketSequence — fixed here via a non-consuming DayTicketSequenceProvider.PeekUpcoming instead of permanently avoiding a live system; the probabilistic-selection layer (vs. the old deterministic "earliest N") is a new design choice made this session, not a restoration of prior behavior — affects: GameManager.cs, DayTicketSequenceProvider.cs, BoardDistributor.cs, BoardDistributionConfig.cs, DayEditorMetaJson (+ DayEditorModel.cs's editor surface for it), DayContentGenerator.cs, DayEditorBoardTimelinePreview.cs, DaySolvabilityChecker.cs, DayValidator.cs — 2026-08-14

D-002: Remove DaySolvabilityChecker and DayValidator's solvability pre-check entirely (Day Editor's Save-button gate keeps only the ticketSequence.Count == ticketsRequiredForDay check + RetryVariant recursion); delete DayEditorBoardTimelinePreview.cs and DayContentGenerator's per-step board simulation (RegenerateBoardTimeline, EnsureSolvable, and the round-robin delivery/spawn loop inside GenerateCore) — rationale: D-001's live reconnect means "is this Day completable" is now guaranteed BY CONSTRUCTION every OnOrderPlaced call at runtime, not something to pre-validate from a per-step authored schedule; DaySolvabilityChecker's counting math assumed exactly that kind of schedule (a ticket's deadline is satisfied by supply spawned at specific TriggerStepIndex values) and produces nonsensical results once BoardTimeline editing is restricted to Day Start (TriggerStepIndex == -1) only — every demand across the whole Day would need to be pre-supplied at Day Start, permanently failing validation for any real Day. Confirmed with the user (AskUserQuestion, chose "Kaldır") rather than deferring to the roadmap's original Phase 5 slot, since D-001's own Phase 4 (BoardTimeline editing restricted to -1) cannot ship coherently without this — affects: DaySolvabilityChecker.cs (deleted), DayValidator.cs, DayContentGenerator.cs, DayEditorBoardTimelinePreview.cs (deleted), DayEditorModel.cs, DayEditorWindow.cs — 2026-08-14

D-003: Remove the per-Day authored "retry variant" feature entirely — `DayDefinition.RetryVariant` (property + constructor parameter), the `hasRetryVariant`/`retryVariant` JSON sentinel pair on `DayRuntimeJson`, `DayCatalogParser`'s recursive variant resolution, `DayValidator`'s recursive `"RetryVariant: "`-prefixed validation, `DayCatalogNavigator.GetEffectiveDay` (the whole method, its only reason to exist), `GameManager.isRetryAttempt` (so `CurrentDay` is a plain `GetDayAt` again), and the Day Editor's "Retry Variant" foldout together with the `IsTopLevel` flag and `FromDayJson`'s `isTopLevel` parameter that existed solely to support a nested variant model — rationale: requested directly by the user ("projedeki retry variant özelliğinin hepsini kaldırır mısın"); the feature's only ever-realized runtime effect was swapping `TicketsRequiredForDay` on a retry, it carried a `JsonUtility` null-sentinel workaround (roadmap Risk 8) and five levels of empty nested blobs in every authored Day file, and CLAUDE.md Section 4's "difficulty scale-down on life loss" was never answered so nothing shipped depended on it. NOTE: this deletes the mechanism that open question would most naturally have used — a future answer to it starts from scratch rather than from `RetryVariant`. The retry *flow* is untouched: `GameManager.RetryDay()` (free life-loss path) and `RetryCompletedDay()` (voluntary star redo) both still replay the Day itself at unchanged difficulty — affects: DayDefinition.cs, DayJson.cs, DayCatalogParser.cs, DayValidator.cs, DayCatalogNavigator.cs, GameManager.cs, DayEditorModel.cs, DayEditorTicketCardPreview.cs, day_00.json, day_01.json, 7 EditMode suites (DayValidatorTests, DayCatalogNavigatorTests, DayCatalogParserTests, DayEditorModelConverterTests, DayContentGeneratorTests, DayFileIOTests, DayTicketSlotManagerIntegrationTests) — 2026-08-14

D-004: Board-distribution balancing is authored PER DAY in Day JSON (`runtime.boardDistribution`) and is the single runtime authority; `BoardDistributionConfig` is demoted to a Day-Editor seed asset (new-Day starting values + its Inspector preview) and leaves the runtime path entirely. Mechanically: a plain `BoardDistributionSettings` value object in Data replaces the ScriptableObject in `BoardDistributor`'s constructor; `DayCatalogParser` builds it from the Day file and hands it over via `DayDefinition.BoardDistribution`; `GameManager` rebuilds the distributor per Day from `CurrentDay.BoardDistribution` and no longer serializes a config reference; `BoardDistributionConfig.CloneWithOverrides` is deleted — rationale: requested by the user ("bu ayarlar Day Editör kısmında olsun ve bölümün JSON kısmına yazılsın", and explicitly "override kısmını da kaldır çünkü override gibi olmayacak"), so there is no base/override layer — every Day carries a complete block. The per-Day fields already existed but had been placed in `editorMeta`, the one section `DayCatalogParser` is contractually blind to (see the class comment on `DayJson`), so all eight were authored, saved and read by nothing at all; moving them under `runtime` is what makes them real. The plain-settings form (rather than `Instantiate`-ing the asset per Day and overwriting its fields) was chosen because the clone would need explicit `Destroy` on every Day change and retry — one leaked ScriptableObject per transition otherwise — and because CLAUDE.md Section 5 requires the Food Distribution Module to carry no Unity dependency; clamping moved with it into `BoardDistributionSettings`' constructor, which is now the single enforcement point for those bounds. NOTE: this changes gameplay. `BoardDistributionConfig.asset` predates four of the eight fields and reads them at the CLR default, which had silently disabled D-001's urgent-ticket guarantee (`urgentTimeThresholdSeconds` 10 → 0) and flattened its arrival-weighted lottery (`earlyTicketWeightDecay` 0.5 → 0); `day_00`/`day_01` were migrated with the DESIGNED values, not the asset's current ones, after confirming with the user — affects: BoardDistributionSettings.cs (new), BoardDistributionConfig.cs, BoardDistributor.cs, DayJson.cs, DayDefinition.cs, DayCatalogParser.cs, GameManager.cs, DayEditorModel.cs, day_00.json, day_01.json, BoardDistributionTests, BoardDistributionConfigCloneWithOverridesTests (deleted), DayCatalogParserTests, DayEditorModelConverterTests, DayValidatorTests, DayContentGeneratorTests, DayTicketSlotManagerIntegrationTests — 2026-08-17

D-005: The play-time half of `TicketGenerationConfig` — the three patience time limits and `upcomingQueueSize` — is authored per Day in Day JSON (`runtime.ticketRuntime`) and is the single runtime authority for those four values; the generation PROBABILITIES (side/drink inclusion, modification count/direction, main-dish weights) stay where they are, because a Day's `ticketSequence` is rolled at authoring time and baked into the file, so those knobs have already done their work before the Day is ever played. Mechanically: a plain `TicketRuntimeSettings` in Data carries the four values; `TicketRuntimeSettingsExtensions` in Core carries the Impatient/Normal/Patient switch (Data must not reference `Core.PatienceType`, the same constraint `EconomyConfig` already documents), replacing `TicketGenerationConfigExtensions`, which is deleted; `DayCatalogParser` resolves the block onto `DayDefinition.TicketRuntime`; `TicketEntryFactory` and `DayTicketSequenceProvider` take the settings instead of the config asset; `GameManager` reads the lookahead from `CurrentDay.TicketRuntime`. `TicketGenerationConfig` survives — unlike `BoardDistributionConfig` in D-004 — because it still owns the names database (a TextAsset reference that cannot live in Day JSON) and the generation knobs, and it gains `ToRuntimeSettings()` as the authoring-side seed. NOTE: the plan called for making `TicketFactory` per-Day as well; reading the code showed that is unnecessary — at play time `TicketFactory` is only a customer-name picker (`Create`/`PickRandomPatienceType` are authoring-only), and the name pool is not per-Day balancing, so it stays session-scoped. Unlike D-004 this changes no behaviour: `TicketGenerationConfig.asset` carried all four values (45/90/150/10) and both Days were migrated with exactly those — affects: TicketRuntimeSettings.cs (new), TicketRuntimeSettingsExtensions.cs (new), TicketGenerationConfigExtensions.cs (deleted), TicketGenerationConfig.cs, TicketFactory.cs, DayJson.cs, DayDefinition.cs, DayCatalogParser.cs, DayTicketSequenceProvider.cs, TicketEntryFactory.cs, GameManager.cs, DayEditorModel.cs, day_00.json, day_01.json, TicketRuntimeSettingsExtensionsTests (new), TicketGenerationConfigExtensionsTests (deleted), TicketEntryFactoryTests, DayTicketSequenceProviderTests, DayCatalogParserTests, DayEditorModelConverterTests, DayTicketSlotManagerIntegrationTests, DayValidatorTests, DayContentGeneratorTests — 2026-08-17

D-006: The ticket-GENERATION settings (side/drink inclusion chance, fallback modification-count lambda, modification-addition chance, main-dish weights) are authored per Day as a complete block in `editorMeta.ticketGeneration`, with no override layer: the `hasTicketGenerationOverride` toggle and the `...Override` field suffixes are gone, and `TicketGenerationConfig.CloneWithOverrides` (nullable params, inherit-what-you-omit) becomes `CloneForDayGeneration` (all five required). `modificationAdditionChance` and `mainDishWeights` were previously not per-Day at all and are added, so the record is complete; weights are stored as food ids and resolved via `FoodCatalog` (`DayContentGenerator.ResolveMainDishWeights`), dropping ids whose `FoodItemConfig` is gone, the same stance `ResolveFoodPool` takes. `MainDishWeight` gains a public constructor so a Day's resolved weights can be built in code — rationale: the user's stated reason for keeping these in the Day file is being able to return to a Day later, SEE which settings it was generated with, and re-generate from them; a partial record ("hangi ayarla yapılmış" minus two of the knobs) would not serve that. These stay in `editorMeta` rather than moving under `runtime` — unlike D-004/D-005 — because a Day's `ticketSequence` is rolled at authoring time and baked into the file, so the probabilities have already done their work before the Day is ever played; `DayCatalogParser`'s contract of never reading `editorMeta` is therefore preserved, and `ParseAll_EditorMetaFieldsIgnored_DoesNotAffectResolution` still enforces it. Deliberate asymmetry with D-004/D-005: a missing block here is NOT fatal (the runtime blocks drop the Day; this one falls back to the seed asset's real values and is written out on the next Save), because runtime correctness does not depend on it and an old Day must stay openable. The Instantiate-clone is also kept here, unlike D-004, because `TicketFactory` needs `namesDatabase` — a TextAsset reference that cannot live in Day JSON — and this path runs only at authoring time and destroys the clone in a `finally`, so there is no per-Day runtime lifetime to leak. No behaviour change: both Days were migrated with the asset's exact values (0.9 / 0.9 / 1.69 / 0.5 / burger weight 1 lambda 2; the weight entry's GUID resolved to Food_Burger, which both Days already serve) — affects: TicketGenerationConfig.cs, DayJson.cs, DayContentGenerator.cs, DayEditorModel.cs, day_00.json, day_01.json, TicketGenerationConfigCloneForDayGenerationTests (replaces TicketGenerationConfigCloneWithOverridesTests), DayContentGeneratorTests, DayCatalogParserTests, DayEditorModelConverterTests — 2026-08-17

D-007: Delete `BoardDistributionConfig` outright — the ScriptableObject class, its `.asset`, and `BoardDistributionConfigEditor` — and seed a new Day's board-distribution balancing from the highest-numbered existing Day instead. `GuaranteedTicketCountMode` moves to `BoardDistributionSettings.cs` (same namespace, so nothing that uses the enum changed), and the leak-distribution preview that only that custom editor drew moves into `DayEditorSettingsPreviews`, shared with the Day Editor and with `TicketGenerationConfigEditor`'s main-dish preview — rationale: after D-004 took the asset off the runtime path its only remaining consumer was its own inspector preview, i.e. a preview of numbers that no longer drove anything; the user asked for it to go ("SOlara gerek kalmadıysa silsek ya"). Seeding from the previous Day (user's choice among three options, 2026-08-17) keeps the numbers in DATA rather than in code, which the root CLAUDE.md invariant requires and which a coded default would have quietly broken — the coded defaults on `DayEditorBoardDistribution` now only apply to the very first Day of an empty catalog. It also matches how Days are actually authored: the next Day usually starts from the last one's balance. Only the three balancing blocks are copied; ticket sequence, Day Start board, star thresholds and food selection are not, since those are what distinguishes one Day from another and inheriting them would disguise an empty new Day as a finished one. `TicketGenerationConfig` survives this deletion for the reasons in D-006 (it owns `namesDatabase` and the generation knobs) and still seeds a new Day's generation/runtime blocks. NOTE: `SampleScene.unity` still carries a `boardDistributionConfig:` line (line 3003) left from the field removed in D-004; with the asset gone it is a dangling GUID on a property no class declares, which Unity drops the next time the scene is saved. The scene was deliberately not hand-edited: it is the project's only scene and K1, and one line of cosmetic cleanup does not justify editing serialized YAML by hand — affects: BoardDistributionConfig.cs (deleted), BoardDistributionConfig.asset (deleted), BoardDistributionConfigEditor.cs (deleted), BoardDistributionSettings.cs, DayEditorSettingsPreviews.cs (new), DayEditorModel.cs, DayEditorWindow.cs, TicketGenerationConfigEditor.cs, SampleScene.unity (stale reference, left for Unity to drop) — 2026-08-17

D-008: The Day Complete popup's 3-star rating is computed from lives lost that day -- 0 lost = 3 stars, 1 = 2, 2 = 1, 3 or more = 0 -- instead of comparing the day's earned score against per-Day authored thresholds. `startingLives` drops from 5 to 3 so the mapping covers the whole reachable range without a gap, the rule moves out of `DayCompletePopupView` into `DayLifecycleManager.StarCount` (which already owns the failure counter, and where it can be tested), and `star1Threshold`/`star2Threshold`/`star3Threshold` are deleted from Day JSON, `DayDefinition`, `GameManager`, the Day Editor and `DayValidator` along with the two warnings about them -- rationale: requested by the user ("elle ayarlamak yerine..."), and the thresholds were a per-Day balancing chore that nobody had actually done: both shipped Days sat at 0/0/0, which awarded 3 stars for any result at all. Lives-based rating needs no authoring per Day and scales with whatever difficulty a Day happens to have. NOTE this changes a rule CLAUDE.md marked as locked, on the user's decision, and it has two consequences worth keeping visible: (1) stars now measure accuracy alone -- speed and patience multipliers still drive the money earned but no longer affect the rating, so there is no star incentive to play fast; (2) 3 starting lives instead of 5 is a real difficulty increase, chosen deliberately over leaving a 3-and-4-losses gap in the mapping. The 3+ case is only reachable through a paid continue (which refills lives but deliberately leaves the failure counter alone) and floors at 0 stars: the day still completes and still pays out, it just earns nothing to show. The GDD .docx is not updated here -- binary file, left to the user -- affects: DayLifecycleManager.cs, DayCompletePopupView.cs, GameManager.cs, DayJson.cs, DayDefinition.cs, DayCatalogParser.cs, DayValidator.cs, DayEditorModel.cs, GameConfig.asset, day_00.json, day_01.json, DayLifecycleManagerTests, DayValidatorTests, DayEditorModelConverterTests, ExpoTheExplorer/CLAUDE.md — 2026-08-17

D-009: Remove the player XP / Level system from the game entirely — `LevelManager` (XP calculation, level-up loop, commit/discard/revert of progress), `LevelProgressionConfig` + its `.asset` (XP-per-item, the three patience XP multipliers, the `XpToNextLevel` threshold table), `GameState.Xp`/`GameState.Level` with their `XpChanged`/`LevelChanged` event buses and constructor seeds, the `XpBarView` and `LevelView` HUD components together with the whole `LevelUI` GameObject subtree in `SampleScene` (level badge, XP bar background/fill/frame, level count text — 6 GameObjects, 24 YAML blocks), `GameManager`'s XP wiring (the `levelProgressionConfig` serialized field, the profile load in `Awake`, the XP award in `OnTicketDelivered`, the `OnDayCompleted`/`OnDayRetried` handlers and their subscriptions, and the `dayStartProfile` half of `CaptureDayStartSnapshot`), and `ProgressionSystemTests` — rationale: requested directly by the user ("XP sistemine dair her şeyi siler misin, Level sistemi olmayacak artık oyunda"). Three consequences worth keeping visible: (1) **patience is now a one-effect stat** — the XP multiplier was its second effect, so patience type feeds nothing but the ticket's time limit, which sharpens GDD open question 22 rather than closing it; (2) **the game persists nothing at all** — Xp/Level was the only state ever written to disk, so every session now starts from `GameState`'s constructor, Day 0 included; (3) the powerup design's "granted on level-up" earn path is void, and powerups (already deferred) will need a new trigger if that scope reopens. `PlayerProfile`/`PlayerProfileStore` and the `ProgressionSystem` assembly are deliberately KEPT rather than deleted with the rest: they are the save *boundary*, not XP-specific, and two queued tasks land in exactly that file — persisting `CurrentDayIndex` (DaySystem_Roadmap Q4) and the wallet plus a schema version (`economy-plan.md` Adım 2). `PlayerProfile` is therefore an empty `[Serializable]` class today and `PlayerProfileStore` has no caller; `PlayerProfileStoreTests` was re-pointed from payload assertions to file behavior (missing/empty/corrupt/round-trip/directory creation, with fallback identity via `AreSame`) so that coverage survives the empty payload. Two `GameStateTests` cases that happened to sit on Xp/Level were re-pointed at SoftMoney rather than deleted — what they guard is the publish-only-on-change setter pattern shared by every reactive `GameState` field. An existing `player_profile.json` in `persistentDataPath` is simply never read again; no migration was written for it — affects: GameState.cs, GameManager.cs, DayCompletePopupView.cs, PlayerProfile.cs, PlayerProfileStore.cs, LevelManager.cs (deleted), LevelProgressionConfig.cs + .asset (deleted), LevelView.cs (deleted), XpBarView.cs (deleted), SampleScene.unity, ProgressionSystemTests (deleted), PlayerProfileStoreTests, GameStateTests, ExpoTheExplorer/CLAUDE.md, GDD (v1.0), docs/LevelSystem_Roadmap.md (deleted), docs/DaySystem_Roadmap.md, blueprint.md, fingerprint.md, economy-plan.md, day-config-plan.md — 2026-08-18

D-010: SoftMoney and Gems get a single, compiler-enforced writer, a day attempt becomes economically atomic, and the wallet becomes the only persisted state. Mechanically: a new `Wallet` in ProgressionSystem owns every balance change (`EarnSoftMoney`, `TrySpendSoftMoney`, `TrySpendGems`, `CaptureDayStart`, `RevertToDayStart`, `ApplyPersistedBalances`); `GameState`'s two balance setters drop from public to `internal` and a new `Core/AssemblyInfo.cs` names ProgressionSystem (plus `Tests.EditMode`) as the only friend assemblies, so Bootstrap, LivesSystem and UI cannot assign a balance at all -- `State.SoftMoney = x` outside those two is a build error. `Wallet` keeps a day-start snapshot of both currencies and a per-day spend ledger; `RevertToDayStart` applies `max(0, dayStartBalance - spentThisDay)` to both. `PlayerProfile` gains `Version` + `SoftMoney` + `Gems`, `PlayerProfileStore` owns `CurrentVersion = 1`, stamps it on every Save, decides the file location itself (`persistentDataPath/player_profile.json`, keeping System.IO out of Bootstrap) and on Load accepts versions 1..CurrentVersion while refusing 0 and anything newer. `GameManager` loses its own `dayStartSoftMoney` field and `CaptureDayStartSnapshot`, gains a `PlayerProfileStore`, and re-creates the `OnDayRetried` and `OnDayCompleted` handlers plus their subscriptions -- both had been deleted by D-009 along with the XP discard/commit they also performed — rationale: three separate audit findings shared one root cause, that nothing owned the currencies. SoftMoney had four writers (GameManager's payout and its RetryCompletedDay rollback, LivesManager's two paid Continues), violating the root CLAUDE.md single-writer invariant; a failed day KEPT the money it had earned, so deliberately losing a day was a way to farm income; and `RetryCompletedDay` handed back a paid Continue. The atomic-day contract ("a day's economic result is provisional until the day completes: earnings are taken back, spending is never refunded") closes the second and third at once and is the same shape the removed XP system already had. The `max(0, ...)` clamp is the user's decision D1, chosen over the alternative of barring Continue purchases funded by the current day's earnings -- one fewer rule for the player, at the cost that a player who funds a Continue that way can end a failed day poorer than they started it, though never in debt. The version-range check is deliberately NOT the plan's "reject anything != 1": that would delete a real player's v1 wallet the day a v2 field is added, whereas an older file is readable and simply lacks the newer fields; version 0 stays refused because an unversioned file's numbers could mean anything and are indistinguishable from a genuine 0 balance, which is the entire reason the invariant demands a version field. NOTE the ledger and snapshots reset only in `CaptureDayStart` (day start / day advance), never on a retry, so two Continues bought across two failed attempts of one day are both still spent and both measured against that day's single baseline. NOTE also `CurrentDayIndex` is deliberately NOT persisted (DaySystem_Roadmap Q4 is a gameplay decision left to the user); the versioned schema makes adding it later cheap, since an existing file reads the missing field as 0, which is exactly "start at Day 0". Only two places write to disk -- `DayCompleted` and `RetryCompletedDay`, the latter because it must correct a figure a completed day already banked -- so quitting mid-day loses that day's earnings by design — affects: Wallet.cs (new), AssemblyInfo.cs (new), WalletTests.cs (new), GameState.cs, PlayerProfile.cs, PlayerProfileStore.cs, LivesManager.cs, ExpoTheExplorer.Systems.LivesSystem.asmdef (+ProgressionSystem reference, new one-directional arrow LivesSystem -> ProgressionSystem), GameManager.cs, LivesSystemTests.cs, PlayerProfileStoreTests.cs, blueprint.md, fingerprint.md, ExpoTheExplorer/CLAUDE.md — 2026-08-18

D-011: A delivery's payout is the summed base prices of the ticket's food (fixed, always paid in full) plus a tip that is a percentage of that same order value, and the tip percentage steps down through three tiers keyed to the fraction of the ticket's OWN time limit still left. The 3-tier speed bonus (Lightning/Fast/Standard, its three multipliers and its two seconds-per-item thresholds) and the entire patience tip-decay machinery (`PatienceDecayStep`, the three authored curves, `ResolvePatienceDecayCoefficient`) are DELETED, not deferred. Mechanically: `FoodItemConfig` gains an authored `basePrice`; `EconomyCalculator.CalculateTip` becomes `CalculatePayout` returning `DeliveryPayoutResult` (`OrderValue`/`Tier`/`TipRate`/`Tip`/`Total`) with a new `TipTier` enum; `EconomyConfig` drops `baseTipPerItem` and the speed knobs and gains `tipRateFull`/`tipRateWarning`/`tipRateCritical` plus `warningRatio`/`criticalRatio`; the two ratios MOVE out of `TicketCardVisualsConfig` (which keeps only the three bar colours and the purely cosmetic `timerSegmentSeconds`) so that one authored value drives both the timer bar's colour and the tip tier, with `TicketCardsView` reading them through a new `GameManager.EconomyConfig` getter; `DayLifecycleManager` renames its private fields but keeps its public property names so `DayCompletePopupView` needed no change; `EconomyConfigEditor` was first rewritten as a tier preview and then deleted at the user's request — rationale: requested by the user across several turns. Item count no longer decides what a delivery is worth (a burger, fries and a cola are not interchangeable), which is what `baseTipPerItem` implied. The old speed tiers were removed even though the GDD marked them locked, because their thresholds were computed as seconds-per-item and were therefore invisible on screen, while the timer bar with its colour changes is already on the card -- of two mechanics doing the same job, the visible one was kept. Keying the tiers to a RATIO rather than seconds makes them scale per patience type for free: an Impatient 45s ticket drops a tier every 15s, a Patient 150s one every 50s, which is now the concrete mechanism behind "patience affects money only through the time limit it grants". Putting the ratios on `EconomyConfig` was the user's explicit instruction, twice confirmed; the visuals copy was deleted rather than kept alongside so the colour the player sees and the tip they are paid cannot drift apart, and the Day Editor's card preview turned out not to read those ratios at all, so no editor work was needed. Order value is never scaled by anything, so a late delivery loses tip but never the food's price -- which also means the Day Complete popup's "Tips" row can no longer go negative, a bug the old `TotalTip - BaseTip` subtraction produced whenever a patience coefficient below 1 pulled the total under its own base. NOTE this changes balance: with the authored 20/10/5 rates the spread between a perfect and a last-second delivery on a 21-value order is 25 vs 22, so speed is currently a minor income factor. NOTE also the deleted `EconomyConfigEditor` had warned about two authoring mistakes nothing now checks -- ratios authored so the Warning tier is unreachable, and rates ordered so a LATER delivery pays more — affects: FoodItemConfig.cs, EconomyConfig.cs, EconomyCalculator.cs, TicketCardVisualsConfig.cs, TicketCardsView.cs, GameManager.cs, DayLifecycleManager.cs, EconomyConfigEditor.cs (deleted), EconomySystemTests.cs, DayLifecycleManagerTests.cs, DayTicketSlotManagerIntegrationTests.cs, TicketSystemTests.cs, the nine Food_*.asset price fields, blueprint.md (new arrow TicketSystem -> EconomySystem), fingerprint.md, ExpoTheExplorer/CLAUDE.md, docs/Expo_the_Explorer_GDD_1.md (v0.8 → v1.1) — 2026-08-18

D-012: The game gets a second scene, `MainScreen`, and the day scene gets three working exits to it; `CurrentDayIndex` becomes persisted (profile schema v1 → v2), which closes DaySystem_Roadmap Q4. Mechanically: a new `SceneFlow` in Core holds the two scene names and the two Single-mode loads; `MainScreenView` (Scripts/UI) renders the day number and both balances read straight from `PlayerProfileStore.Load()` and sends the player back into the day; `PlayerProfile` gains `CurrentDayIndex` and `PlayerProfileStore.CurrentVersion` goes to 2; `GameManager` applies the persisted index in `Awake` through a new `ResolveStartingDayIndex` clamp (after the catalog is parsed, before the provider is built off `CurrentDay`), writes it in `SaveProfile`, and gains `ReturnToMainScreenFromCompletedDay` + `ReturnToMainScreenAbandoningDay`; `DayCompletePopupView.goBackButton` and `GameOverPopupView.mainMenuButton` lose their `interactable = false` lines and get listeners; `AdvanceToNextDay` now also saves. The scene itself is produced by a construction script, `MainScreenSceneBuilder` — rationale: requested by the user ("bölüm bitince ana ekrana gidebilmeliyim"), and both buttons had been sitting in `SampleScene` since their popups were built, disabled behind a `// TODO: wire once a menu/day-select scene exists`. Scope was confirmed with the user before any code: a navigation shell (not a day-select map, not a hub with upgrades — no meta CONTENT is designed here, only the flow it will hang off), resume-where-you-left-off, and a separate scene rather than an in-scene panel. **Separate Single-mode scenes** matter beyond taste: the day scene is destroyed on exit, so `GameManager` keeps its "Awake builds the whole Day" shape and its ~dozens of hand-wired Inspector references untouched, and no day runtime lives on behind the main screen. With no persistent scene, **the save file is the hand-off between the two scenes**, which is why `MainScreenView` reads the profile directly — it is a pure reader, so `GameManager` remains the single writer of both balances and the day index. Three sub-decisions worth keeping visible: (1) **"Go Back" advances the day.** The day is finished when that button is reachable, so the next thing the main screen should offer is the next Day. It deliberately does NOT call `AdvanceToNextDay`: only the index has to survive, and that method's board/tray/slot reset would be thrown away as the scene unloads while cascading `TicketAssigned` through views that are already tearing down. (2) **Leaving a failed attempt now WRITES to disk** — `Wallet.RevertToDayStart()` then `SaveProfile()` — which changes the older "a failed day never writes" line in this file's D-010 and in CLAUDE.md. It has to: the new button creates a hole that did not exist while there was nowhere to go, since without the write a Continue bought with **Gems** during an attempt the player then abandons would be silently refunded by walking out, making paid Continues free for anyone who ends up leaving. The write can only ever record money already SPENT, never earned, so it cannot bank a failed day's income — the atomic-day contract is extended to "leaving settles the attempt the way a retry does", not reversed. The day index is deliberately not advanced there, so the main screen still offers that same Day. (3) **The last authored Day gets an answer**: "Next Day" used to return false and just hide the popup, leaving the player on a finished day with no UI at all (the code called this "PR-9 decides"); it now exits to the main screen, whose Play replays that last Day since the index cannot advance. A real "all days finished" screen is content, deliberately not invented here. NOTE the scene is built by an Editor construction script rather than committed as YAML: a Canvas+TMP scene hand-written as serialized YAML is exactly the edit D-007 declined to make, it cannot be reviewed, and a scene file cannot put itself first in Build Settings. The script refuses to overwrite an existing `MainScreen.unity`, so restyling it later is safe. It lives under `Scripts/UI/Editor/` rather than `Assets/Editor/` because it references `MainScreenView` in the predefined Assembly-CSharp, which the `ExpoTheExplorer.Editor` asmdef cannot reference at all. NOTE also `GameManager` is structurally untestable from the EditMode suite (predefined assembly vs asmdef test assembly), so `ResolveStartingDayIndex` and both exit paths carry no test; the profile's v2 round-trip and the v1-reads-as-Day-0 migration do — affects: SceneFlow.cs (new), MainScreenView.cs (new), MainScreenSceneBuilder.cs (new), PlayerProfile.cs, PlayerProfileStore.cs, GameManager.cs, DayCompletePopupView.cs, GameOverPopupView.cs, PlayerProfileStoreTests.cs, MainScreen.unity (created by the script, not committed by hand), EditorBuildSettings.asset (reordered by the script), blueprint.md (new MainScreen system + the first real scene inventory + a Scripts/<Area>/Editor/ folder line), fingerprint.md, ExpoTheExplorer/CLAUDE.md — 2026-08-19

D-013: The HUD Canvas becomes a single prefab (`Assets/Prefabs/UI/HudCanvas.prefab`) instanced in BOTH scenes, showing coins, gems and lives in each, and a new `HudWalletSource` on its root becomes the one place that answers "where does this scene's HUD read its numbers from"; `MainScreenView` gives up its own coins/gems labels so the wallet is displayed once rather than twice. Mechanically: `HudWalletSource` (Scripts/UI) exposes `LiveState`/`SoftMoney`/`Gems`/`Lives`/`MaxLives` and resolves per scene — a `GameManager` if one exists (live `GameState`, reactive, unchanged gameplay behaviour) else `PlayerProfileStore.Load()` for the balances and `GameState.DefaultStartingLives` for lives; `SoftMoneyView`, `GemsView` and `LivesView` drop their `[SerializeField] GameManager` for a `walletSource` reference, subscribe only when `LiveState` is non-null, and read every VALUE through the source so one code path serves both modes; `MainScreenView` loses `softMoneyValueText`/`gemsValueText`; `MainScreenSceneBuilder` stops creating the four labels behind them; and `HudCanvasPrefabSetup` provides the migration — rationale: requested by the user ("bu canvas hem ana menüde hem de oyun sahnesinde olsun") right after they fixed the HUD's own layout. The blocker was that all three views treated `gameManager` as mandatory and bailed with a LogError when null, which is every main-screen instance, since that scene has no `GameManager` by design (D-012). Putting the resolution in ONE component rather than a null-check in each view is what stops three copies of the rule from drifting and three separate profile reads per scene load. **How the GameManager reference is bound was the one contested detail**: a prefab ASSET cannot store a scene reference, so it is either resolved at runtime or carried as a per-scene override. This shipped first as `FindAnyObjectByType` for exactly that reason, and was then changed to a `[SerializeField] GameManager` **on the user's explicit instruction** ("ya o aramasın ben serialize field olarak vereyim") so the binding is visible in the Inspector and theirs to control. The trade-off was stated before the change and is real: the field is a prefab override in the day scene, Apply All from that scene drops it, and an empty field is not an error (it is precisely how the main screen works), so the failure is QUIET — the HUD stops following the live wallet and sits on the save file's numbers for the whole day. Two things answer that: `HudWalletSource.Resolve` logs which of the two modes it resolved to once per scene load, and `HudCanvasPrefabSetup` step 2 writes the reference itself for whichever scene it is run on instead of leaving a manual drag. The views' own `walletSource` references are prefab-INTERNAL and need no override in either scene. Resolution is LAZY, on the first view's `Start`, never in an `Awake`: `GameManager` assigns `State` in its own `Awake` and Unity does not order `Awake` across GameObjects, so resolving earlier would reintroduce exactly the null that the views' long-standing Start-not-Awake rule exists to avoid. Three consequences worth keeping visible: (1) **the main screen's lives label is a constant "3/3"** — lives are never persisted and always start full, so there is no live value to show; the user was told this and chose to display it anyway rather than hide the widget, and it reads as "you will start the next day with 3". `HudWalletSource.Lives` is the single line a persistent-lives design would change. (2) Removing `gameManager` from the three views orphans their serialized values in `SampleScene`; Unity discards the property silently and nothing needs rewiring, but reverting the .cs alone would leave those fields empty. (3) `HudWalletSource` carries no test: it is a MonoBehaviour in the predefined `Assembly-CSharp`, which an asmdef test assembly cannot reference — the same wall `GameManager` sits behind — so both branches are covered by reasoning and a Play-mode pass only. NOTE the migration deliberately ships as TWO menu items (`ExpoTheExplorer > HUD > 1/2`) rather than one automated sweep: a single command would have to switch scenes, and switching forces a save or a discard, which destroys the undo stack and commits changes before the user has reviewed them. Each step touches only the open scene, writes nothing to disk but the prefab asset, and leaves the scene dirty so saving stays the user's call. Step 1 saves the prefab FROM the live object, so the HUD rect values the user authored are what ships — this decision re-parents nothing and re-derives no anchors. NOTE also this task recorded, but did not fix, a tooling bug found on its path: `build_unitymap.py`, `check_blueprint.py` and `build_assetmap.py` all resolve `<project_root>/Assets` while this repo nests the Unity project at `ExpoTheExplorer/Assets`, so `unitymap.md` has been stamping `scenes:0 status: OK` over a project with four scenes; `unitymap.md` now carries an explicit UNMAPPED note naming the cause, and the scene structure for this task was read by parsing `SampleScene.unity` directly — affects: HudWalletSource.cs (new), HudCanvasPrefabSetup.cs (new), SoftMoneyView.cs, GemsView.cs, LivesView.cs (its codemap line also went from MISSING-role/sys: ? to sys: LivesSystem), MainScreenView.cs, MainScreenSceneBuilder.cs, GameState.cs (the D-012 comment fix that had been left pending), HudCanvas.prefab + SampleScene.unity + MainScreen.unity (all three produced by the migration steps, not committed by hand), blueprint.md (first real prefab inventory), unitymap.md, ExpoTheExplorer/CLAUDE.md — 2026-08-19

D-014: `Lives` joins the save file (profile schema v2 → v3, with the project's first real migration) and the three HUD views stop knowing that two data sources exist. Mechanically: `PlayerProfile` gains `Lives`; `PlayerProfileStore.CurrentVersion` goes to 3 and gains `UpgradeToCurrent` (pre-v3 files get `Lives = GameState.DefaultStartingLives`) plus `Defaults()` (a brand-new player: zero money, FULL lives); `LivesManager` gains `ApplyPersistedLives`, clamped to 1..MaxLives, mirroring `Wallet.ApplyPersistedBalances`; `GameManager` applies it in `Awake` and writes `Lives` in `SaveProfile`; `HudWalletSource` gains three forwarded `EventBus<int>`s (`SoftMoneyChanged`/`GemsChanged`/`LivesChanged`, the last fed by both of GameState's lives events) and its `Lives` now returns the SAVED count off the day scene instead of a constant; `SoftMoneyView`/`GemsView`/`LivesView` lose their `LiveState` branch, their `liveState` field and their knowledge of modes, becoming one subscription plus one render each — rationale: the user asked for coins/gems/lives to be reachable from anywhere with clean code, and the reason the HUD had become ugly was that these values had TWO homes (GameState in a day, the save file on the menu), which forced a branch into three separate views. A ScriptableObject-as-runtime-home refactor was designed for this and REJECTED by the user in favour of "let the main-menu HUD just read the JSON" — the right call: the menu cannot change a balance, so one render there is sufficient, and 15 files of surgery across two K1 files with no runnable tests was a bad trade. What shipped instead is the same idea at a tenth of the cost: one home per question, but the mode choice confined to `HudWalletSource` rather than replicated in the views. Three consequences worth keeping visible: (1) **the main screen's lives label stopped being decorative** — persisting Lives is what made it real, which is also why this task and the lives decision arrived together; (2) **`ReturnToMainScreenAbandoningDay` now refills lives before saving**, and that line is load-bearing: it is only reachable from the Game Over popup with Lives at 0, so persisting that figure would have written a save file the player cannot play out of — dead before the first ticket, permanently. Abandoning resets the attempt exactly as the free Retry does; (3) `RetryCompletedDay`'s `SaveProfile` moved from first to LAST for the same reason — it refills lives, and saving before that wrote the pre-refill count, handing back a half-empty bar to anyone who quit mid-redo. NOTE persisting Lives opened no design question, contrary to my first read: lives ALREADY carried across days (`AdvanceToNextDay` deliberately skips `LivesManager`; only a failed-day retry or a paid Continue refills), so this extends an existing rule across sessions rather than inventing one. NOTE also what was deliberately NOT fixed: the `GameManager` reference on the HUD prefab instance is still a prefab OVERRIDE, so "Apply All" from the day scene still drops it and the symptom is still quiet — only an asset reference removes that, and that was the rejected refactor. `HudWalletSource`'s mode log stays as the tell — affects: PlayerProfile.cs, PlayerProfileStore.cs, LivesManager.cs, GameManager.cs, HudWalletSource.cs, SoftMoneyView.cs, GemsView.cs, LivesView.cs, PlayerProfileStoreTests.cs, LivesSystemTests.cs, ExpoTheExplorer/CLAUDE.md, fingerprint.md — 2026-08-19

D-015: The meta side of the game — the expo grounds the player decorates between days — gets a designed shape and a new `MetaSystem`, built MULTI-LOCATION from the first line. This is the project's first DESIGN-ONLY decision record: no code ships with it, because ExpoTheExplorer/CLAUDE.md's "Meta content … is not designed yet — build the design first, then hang it here" makes the design itself the deliverable, and `.claude/meta-plan.md` is its 11-step execution plan. Six mechanisms, each with the alternative it beat. (1) **One slot = one Image with two sprites.** A catalog entry carries `unownedSprite` and `ownedSprite`, either of which may be empty, so the whole view layer is `image.sprite = active ? owned : unowned; image.enabled = image.sprite != null`. This collapses what looked like four product types into one code path: a cosmetic decor has only an owned sprite; the ruined `StartBuilding` that hides the finished stand has only an UNOWNED one and therefore vanishes when bought; an area expansion (`Square`) is a decor that additionally unlocks slots; a Day-unlocked prop differs only in what decides `active`. The rejected alternative was a `kind` enum with a switch per type — four branches to keep in sync for a two-field data change. (2) **A Day-unlocked prop's openness is DERIVED, never persisted**: `active ⟺ some Day in 0..CurrentDayIndex serves the trigger content`. Because `CurrentDayIndex` names the day about to be played, that scan opens the prop exactly BEFORE the day that needs it, which is what the user asked for ("sonraki bölümde sos vardır, o bölümden önce açılması gerekir") and it needs no `+1`. Persisting the unlocked set was rejected outright: it is a second authority that starts lying the moment a Day is re-authored. The trigger is `DayUnlockTrigger` with four optional fields OR-ed together — `foods` (FoodItemConfig refs), `foodCategories` (FoodCategory), `modifications` (ModificationConfig refs) and a plain `minDayIndex` — because the three props Meta1 actually has each look at a DIFFERENT concept: the fryer at one food (`Food_Fries`), the drinks fridge at a whole category (six beverages, so a seventh added later cannot silently fail to trigger it), and the sauces stand at a set of modifications (`Mod_ExtraKetchup`/`Mayonnaise`/`Mustard` — note `FoodCategory` has no `Sauce` member; sauces are modifications, which is why one trigger shape could not serve all three). Everything is an ASSET REFERENCE, not a string id, so the root invariant on content-never-in-code holds and renames cannot break it. (3) **The wallet keeps its single writer on the main screen, via a `GameSession` extracted from `GameManager` rather than a second construction path.** A shop that spends money needs a `Wallet`, and the main screen has neither `GameManager` nor `GameState` by design (D-012/D-013). `GameManager.Awake`'s first five steps — `GameState`, `Wallet` + `ApplyPersistedBalances`, `LivesManager` + `ApplyPersistedLives`, the Day catalog parse, and the `ResolveStartingDayIndex` clamp — are exactly what the meta screen needs and exactly one contiguous block, so they become `GameSession` and BOTH screens construct it. Writing a separate `MetaScreenController` that re-implemented those five was designed and REJECTED: four load-bearing ordering rules live in that block (the wallet must exist before `ApplyPersistedBalances` because GameState's setters are `internal`; `LivesManager` before anything that can cost a life; the catalog before the clamp, which needs its `Count`; and `ApplyPersistedBalances` re-takes the day-start snapshot), and duplicating them across two files is the exact failure the single-writer invariant exists to prevent. Moving `GameManager` itself to the main screen — the user's question — was rejected for a concrete reason: `Awake` ends in `ApplyDayStartBoardPreSeed()` + `TicketSlotManager.FillEmptySlots()` and `Update()` runs `TicketSlotManager.Tick`, so the day would RUN behind the shop, timing tickets out and costing lives; a `DontDestroyOnLoad` variant fails differently, since every view in `SampleScene` holds a `[SerializeField] GameManager` that a carried-in object cannot satisfy, which would reverse the user's explicit D-013 instruction. `GameSession` being plain C# in an asmdef also closes a coverage gap D-012 recorded: `ResolveStartingDayIndex`'s clamp becomes EditMode-testable for the first time. Alongside it, `player_profile.json` gets ONE writer class (`ProfileSaver`) that both `GameManager` and the meta screen call with a whole profile, so the file cannot be written with a stale day index or lives; and `HudWalletSource` binds to a `GameSession` provider instead of a concrete `GameManager`, which makes the main-screen HUD live after a purchase and follows D-013/D-014's own rule to extend the source rather than branch inside a view. (4) **No third scene**: the grounds render inside `MainScreen`, which the GDD already designated ("hang it here") and which leaves D-012's two-scene shape untouched — a third scene would add a second save-file hand-off. (5) **Slot positions are normalized (0..1) to the background rect**, not pixels: `Main.png` is 853×1844 (0.463, a phone portrait ratio) but real devices run 0.42–0.56, so pixel offsets drift under aspect-fill while normalized ones do not — and it lets locations with differently-sized backgrounds share one data shape. 19 slots are too many to hand-author, so an Editor placement tool writes positions back into the asset, the same authoring-tool pattern DayEditor and MainScreenSceneBuilder already use. (6) **Location is a first-class entity and decor belongs to it** (`MetaCatalog.locations` → `MetaLocation{ id, displayName, backgroundSprite, unlockAtDayIndex, items[] }`), because the user stated a second restaurant is coming and that which props are cosmetic vs. Day-unlocked "metadan metaya değişebilir". Consequences: the persistence key is `$"{location.Id}.{item.Id}"` COMPUTED by the resolver, so assets keep clean local ids while uniqueness is structural and two locations can carry a same-named decor; a location's unlock is derived from `CurrentDayIndex >= unlockAtDayIndex`, the same shape as (2), so no save field; the wallet is SHARED across locations while decor is not; which location is being viewed is UI state, not saved (the screen opens on the newest unlocked one); and a location is self-contained — a `requiresAreaId` pointing into another location is a validation error. Doing this now costs a wrapper level in the catalog and a location parameter on the resolver; doing it later would have meant changing the schema of already-saved ownership keys, i.e. a save migration. Deliberately NOT built: any gameplay effect (decor is purely cosmetic — no income multiplier, no lives), Gems anywhere on the meta side (they stay Continue-only), free placement (fixed slots, the user's choice), a day-select map, per-location economies, and decor transferable between locations. Two design consequences worth keeping visible: a Day-unlocked prop's trigger reads GLOBAL content rather than a location's own timeline, so a location unlocking at Day 12 opens with its fridge already present if drinks entered at Day 3 (correct — the new restaurant is built with everything already on the menu); and the "old location stays visitable and decorable" rule is what removes the sunk-cost trap that a hard move would have created, where every coin spent before the move stops being visible and the rational player therefore ignores the meta system entirely — rationale: requested by the user ("şimdi meta oyunu implement etmeye başlıycaz"), with the six locked answers gathered before any design (fixed slots; SoftMoney only and price is the only gate; Day-CONTENT-driven required props; Square as an area expansion; locations accumulate rather than replace; a Day number unlocks a location). The art set is already organized per location (`Assets/Art/Meta1/`, 20 PNGs), which is what surfaced (6) before any code existed — affects: THIS TASK wrote only maps — blueprint.md (new `MetaSystem` system line + its two arrows, `MainScreen` gains a third arrow, and the folder layout gains the `Scripts/Bootstrap/` and `Scripts/Systems/<System>/` lines it had never carried), decisions.md, index.md, .claude/meta-plan.md. PLANNED code surface, none of it written yet: MetaCatalog.cs + MetaCatalog.asset (new), MetaResolver.cs + MetaPurchase.cs + MetaSystem asmdef (new), MetaSystemTests.cs (new), GameSession.cs + GameSessionTests.cs (new), ProfileSaver.cs (new), MetaBoardView.cs + MetaShopView.cs (new), a Meta placement Editor tool (new), PlayerProfile.cs + PlayerProfileStore.cs (schema v3 → v4), GameManager.cs, HudWalletSource.cs, MainScreen.unity, and the Meta1 sprite import settings. `shards.json` is deliberately unchanged: `Scripts/Systems/MetaSystem/**` matches no pattern and lands in the `core` catch-all, exactly like every other `Scripts/Systems/*` folder on disk — 2026-08-20

D-016: A meta prop has exactly ONE sprite and is simply absent until it is active; D-015's `unownedSprite`/`ownedSprite` pair drops to a single `sprite`. Mechanically: `MetaItemDefinition` loses `unownedSprite`, renames `ownedSprite` to `sprite` (matching `FoodItemConfig.sprite`) and drops one constructor parameter; `MetaCatalogValidator`'s "has neither sprite" error becomes "has no sprite"; `MetaCatalogValidatorTests` loses `Validate_ItemWithOnlyUnownedSprite_IsFine` (the case being removed) and its realistic-Meta1 fixture swaps the ruined-building entry for an ordinary bought `HotdogStand` — rationale: requested by the user ("unowned owned muhabbetini kaldıralım sadece alınca gözüksün, spriteları da ona göre düzenleyeyim ben"). The second sprite existed for exactly one prop: the ruin that hides the finished stand baked into `Main.png`, so that BUYING it made it vanish. Expressing that in the ART instead — the background carries the ruin, and the stand becomes a normal prop drawn over it — produces the same thing on screen with one less mechanism, and the art is where "what does the ground look like before anything is built" belongs anyway. The whole view layer is now `image.enabled = active`. NOTE what this genuinely gives up: the system can no longer express "buying this removes it" AT ALL. That was the only field capable of it, so a future prop needing that behaviour needs a new mechanism rather than a value — worth knowing before someone reaches for it and finds nothing. NOTE also this changes an art obligation the user accepted: `Main.png` currently ships the FINISHED stand, so it has to be re-exported with the ruin instead, and a stand sprite added; until that happens the catalog simply has no entry for either, since Adım 2 shipped the schema and not the content. Cheap to make now — three files written this same session, no resolver, no UI and no asset reading them yet, so there was no caller to break; the same edit after Adım 7 would have touched the view and the placement tool as well — affects: MetaCatalog.cs, MetaCatalogValidator.cs, MetaCatalogValidatorTests.cs, codemap-core.md, meta-plan.md (K1 rewritten), Art/Meta1/Main.png + a stand sprite (the user's art work, not done here) — 2026-08-20

D-017: A Day-unlocked meta prop appears at ONE authored day index and nothing else; D-015's content-driven trigger is deleted. Mechanically: the whole `DayUnlockTrigger` class goes (its `foods`/`foodCategories`/`modifications`/`minDayIndex` fields, `IsEmpty`, `HasMissingReferences`), replaced by a single `MetaItemDefinition.unlockAtDayIndex` -- the same shape and meaning `MetaLocation.unlockAtDayIndex` already had, so props and locations now unlock by exactly one rule; `MetaCatalogValidator` loses the empty-trigger error and the deleted-reference warning, keeping only the "priced but not for sale" warning; `MetaCatalogValidatorTests` drops five trigger tests for three; and `MetaCatalog.cs` stops referencing `FoodItemConfig`, `FoodCategory` and `ModificationConfig` altogether — rationale: requested by the user ("sadece hangi günden önce açılacağını seçebilelim, böyle çok gereksiz karmaşa var ve gereksiz arama tarama var"). They are right about the ratio: D-015 bought "the fridge appears exactly when drinks join the menu, and un-appears if you re-author them away" at the price of four OR-ed trigger fields, a `DayContentFacts` scan over every Day up to the player's, and a `MetaSystem -> DaySystem` dependency. **Three things vanish with it, not one:** `meta-plan.md`'s entire Adım 6 (the content-scan step), the blueprint arrow to DaySystem, and open questions M3/M4/M4b (which modifications count as sauce, whether the fridge keys on a category or six ids, whether the fryer keys on `Food_Fries` or all Sides) -- all three were only questions BECAUSE the unlock read content. What is genuinely given up: the two numbers can now drift. Move sauces from Day 5 to Day 9 and the sauces stand still appears at Day 5 until someone edits the catalog too, and no validator can catch it -- `Data` has no access to the Day catalog by construction, so the check is unwriteable rather than merely unwritten. One authored number per prop, visible in the Inspector, is the mitigation. NOTE the index is a CATALOG POSITION, 0-based, not the day number the player sees (which is index + 1, per `MainScreenView`): "waiting before their fifth day" is authored as 4, and the tooltip says so, because this is exactly the confusion `fingerprint.md` already records between `CurrentDayIndex` and a Day file's own `dayIndex`. NOTE also `MetaUnlockKind` SURVIVES: with two int fields (`price`, `unlockAtDayIndex`) the discriminator is what says which one governs, and inferring it ("price > 0 means it is for sale") would make a mis-authored 0 silently change a prop's kind. Free to do now -- the three files were written the same session, the `.asset` the user created is still empty (`locations: []`), and no resolver, UI or Editor tool reads any of it yet, so there was no caller to break and no authored data to migrate — affects: MetaCatalog.cs, MetaCatalogValidator.cs, MetaCatalogValidatorTests.cs, blueprint.md (MetaSystem loses its DaySystem arrow), meta-plan.md (K2 rewritten, Adım 6 deleted, M3/M4/M4b removed), codemap-core.md, index.md — 2026-08-20

D-018: The meta catalog stays a ScriptableObject and gets a custom `MetaEditorWindow` to author it; it does NOT move to JSON like Day content. Mechanically: two new files under `Assets/Editor/` -- `MetaEditorWindow` (an `OdinMenuEditorWindow`: one tree node per location, a toolbar carrying the auto-discovered catalog field and the validation counts) and `MetaLocationLayoutGUI` (the layout canvas: the location's background drawn at its real aspect with every prop DRAGGABLE on top) -- plus one comment line on `DayEditorSpriteGUI`, whose `DrawTexCoords` is reused as a third consumer. No asmdef changes: `ExpoTheExplorer.Editor` already references `ExpoTheExplorer.Data` — rationale: the user asked for Day-Editor-style authoring instead of clicking through nested Inspector lists ("day editör gibi editörde dediklerini yapsak ya SO yerine"), and then, once the storage question was separated from the UI question, chose to keep the SO ("SO ile tutalım o zaman editörde düzenleyelim"). **Separating those two questions was the substance of this decision**: a custom EditorWindow does not require JSON — the deleted `EconomyConfigEditor` drew a ScriptableObject the same way — so the authoring gain was available without touching storage at all, which also meant D-015's schema and D-016/D-017's narrowings survived untouched instead of being rewritten hours after they shipped. JSON was considered seriously and rejected for a concrete reason, recorded here because the question will be asked again: Day JSON references foods by string id BECAUSE foods live elsewhere and are shared across every Day, and `FoodCatalog.GetById` is that bridge — the JSON does not avoid a ScriptableObject, it depends on one. Meta props are not shared; each belongs to exactly one location, so the id indirection would buy nothing while adding a mismatch class (a prop id in JSON with no entry in the sprite catalog) that the SO cannot have, since there the sprite sits beside the price. Sprites cannot live in JSON at all, which the project already recorded when `TicketGenerationConfig` survived deletion for owning `namesDatabase`. Three implementation choices worth keeping visible: (1) **the window edits through `SerializedObject`, not by assigning to the plain `MetaLocation` objects.** They live inside the SO's list, so a direct write changes memory and leaves the asset unmarked -- the edit would survive until the next domain reload and then silently vanish. It also buys Unity's undo and Ctrl+S rather than a hand-rolled Save, which is the opposite division of labour from `DayEditorWindow` (mutable model, JSON on Save) and deliberate: there is no second copy of meta data to keep in step. ONE `ApplyModifiedProperties` per repaint is what makes a drag undo as a single gesture instead of one step per mouse-move event. (2) **Odin draws only the chrome and the tree; every field goes through Unity's own property drawer**, so the page cannot drift from whatever the schema does next. (3) **"Depth from Y" is a button, not automatic** -- deriving sort order on every drag would wipe any hand-set exception the moment its prop is nudged, and exceptions are the whole reason that field is authored rather than computed. NOTE the canvas flips every vertical conversion (GUI space Y grows downward, a normalized UI position grows upward) and derives prop size as sprite-pixels x the background's fit scale, mirroring the runtime -- so the preview stays a preview rather than a second opinion (D-166 later added a per-prop `scale` multiplier on top and moved this derivation into the shared `MetaLayout.PropSize`, which is what kept that sentence true). NOTE also `sys: MetaSystem` rather than a new `MetaEditor` system, which is an asymmetry with blueprint's `DayEditor`: two files do not earn a system, and it can be split out if the window grows. NOTE finally this ABSORBS `meta-plan.md` Adım 7's separate "Editor placement tool" line item -- there is no second tool to build — affects: MetaEditorWindow.cs (new), MetaLocationLayoutGUI.cs (new), DayEditorSpriteGUI.cs (comment: third consumer), codemap-editor.md, meta-plan.md (Adım 2.5 added, Adım 7's tool item absorbed) — 2026-08-20

D-019: The meta rules ship as a dependency-free decision core: a new `ExpoTheExplorer.Systems.MetaSystem` assembly referencing `ExpoTheExplorer.Data` and NO system, and D-015's planned `MetaSystem -> ProgressionSystem` arrow is dropped rather than realised. Mechanically: `MetaResolver` answers what is on the grounds (`IsOwned`, `IsLocationUnlocked`, `UnlockedLocations`, `DefaultLocationIndex`, `IsActive`, `IsAreaSatisfied`, `ActiveItems`, each with a list overload beside the asset one) and `MetaPurchase` answers whether a prop can be bought (`MetaPurchaseVerdict{Ok,NotForSale,AlreadyOwned,LocationLocked,AreaLocked,NotEnoughMoney}`, `Evaluate`, `CanBuy`, `ShopItems`); `MetaSystemTests` covers 25 cases; the EditMode test asmdef gains the new reference — rationale: `meta-plan.md` Adım 3, written before any UI so the rules are pinned by tests rather than discovered by playing. **The arrow came off because writing the code showed it was never needed**: the rules take the owned-key set and the SoftMoney balance as PARAMETERS, so the money and the owned-item list keep the single writers they already have in ProgressionSystem (`Wallet`, the profile writer) and the screen's composition root joins the two — the same shape `GameManager` uses to join systems. What that buys is not tidiness but reach: zero system dependencies and zero MonoBehaviour means every rule is exercisable from a test, which is exactly what `GameManager` cannot offer (D-012 recorded that gap). The cost is a rule for callers, stated in the class comment: nothing is looked up, so a caller passing a stale day index gets a confidently wrong answer rather than an exception. Four decisions inside the rules are worth keeping visible. (1) **A verdict, not a bool.** The shop has to say six different things and "you cannot afford it" versus "buy the square first" are different screens; neither is recoverable from true/false. The check ORDER is load-bearing and runs most-fundamental-first — `NotForSale` before anything about money, because reporting `NotEnoughMoney` for a prop that was never purchasable would send a player off to earn money for nothing; and `AreaLocked` before `NotEnoughMoney`, because an area-gated prop's price is irrelevant until the area is owned. (2) **Area gating applies to being ACTIVE, not only to being purchasable.** Owning a prop whose area is unowned should be unreachable, but an author can add `requiresAreaId` to a prop players already have, and then their table would hang over ungravelled grass; tying visibility to the area keeps the picture coherent whatever the catalog does next. An area id naming nothing, or naming a non-area item, counts as UNSATISFIED so the content bug the validator reports stays visible instead of hiding behind a working prop. (3) **`ActiveItems` uses insertion sort, not `List.Sort`**, because `List.Sort` is not stable: two props sharing a `SortOrder` would swap places between calls and the visible symptom is overlapping props flickering. A test pins it. (4) **`ShopItems` deliberately KEEPS unaffordable and area-gated props** and drops only owned and Day-unlocked ones — seeing what is still to come, and what it costs, is most of what a shop is for. NOTE `DefaultLocationIndex` walks back from the END of the catalog rather than picking the highest unlock day: catalog order is authored order and two locations may legitimately share a day; -1 means nothing is unlocked, which `MetaCatalogValidator` already reports as a content error rather than something this class should paper over. NOTE also the tests seed the catalog's private list by REFLECTION rather than through `SerializedObject` the way `EconomySystemTests` does, because the difference is what is being set — floats write in one line each, a list of plain serializable objects would need every field of every nested object walked by name, which is more fragile than the reflection it replaces and would need updating on every schema change; a public setter would exist purely for tests. NOTE finally that this step is verified by COMPILATION only (the assembly and the tests against it, both 0 errors, via temporary csproj files since Unity had not yet generated one for the new asmdef): the Editor holds the project lock, so the suite itself has not been run — affects: ExpoTheExplorer.Systems.MetaSystem.asmdef (new), MetaResolver.cs (new), MetaPurchase.cs (new), MetaSystemTests.cs (new), ExpoTheExplorer.Tests.EditMode.asmdef (+MetaSystem reference), blueprint.md (MetaSystem loses its last dependency arrow), codemap-core.md (3 new lines, plus the Tests.EditMode.asmdef line finished from its auto-added state), meta-plan.md — 2026-08-20

D-020: The props a player has bought join the save file: `PlayerProfile.OwnedMetaItemIds` (a `List<string>` of qualified `"<location>.<item>"` keys) and `PlayerProfileStore.CurrentVersion` goes 3 → 4. Mechanically: the new field plus a `Normalize` step in `PlayerProfileStore` that guarantees a non-null list on every load, four new cases in `PlayerProfileStoreTests`, and the schema line in `ExpoTheExplorer/CLAUDE.md` — rationale: `meta-plan.md` Adım 4; without it a purchase evaporates when the game closes, which makes the whole meta side pointless. **The interesting part is that v4 breaks the pattern the previous three versions established, in the opposite direction to v3.** v1→v3 all reasoned about whether absent-reads-as-ZERO was safe: v2's `CurrentDayIndex` was fine (0 means the first Day), v3's `Lives` was not (0 lives is a dead player), and that asymmetry is why `UpgradeToCurrent` exists. This is the first REFERENCE-typed field, so the question is not what value an absent field reads as but whether it arrives at all — and the safe default is an empty list, which no zero can express. That produced the one decision worth keeping visible: **`Normalize` sits OUTSIDE `UpgradeToCurrent`, and that placement is load-bearing.** `UpgradeToCurrent` returns early once the file is already current, which is correct for a version-specific fix but wrong here, because a CURRENT-version file can still carry an explicit null — hand-edited, half-written, or produced by a future field someone forgets to initialize — and every one of those would reach a caller as a `NullReferenceException` rather than as an empty inventory. It also wraps the fallback returns, since a caller-supplied fallback is an ordinary object someone else built and its list can be null just as easily as a parsed one's. A test uses a literal v4 payload with `"OwnedMetaItemIds":null` precisely to pin that gap, because the obvious v3-payload test passes either way. Three smaller calls: a `List<string>` rather than a `HashSet`, because `JsonUtility` round-trips a HashSet as `"{}"` with no error at all (the same trap `PlayerProfile`'s own comment already documents about auto-properties) and callers wanting set semantics convert on read — `MetaResolver` already takes an `ISet<string>`; the key FORMAT is not decided here, `MetaCatalog.OwnershipKey` stays its single authority and this class only carries strings, because a joined key written down in two places is exactly what one authority prevents; and the field holds PURCHASES only — whether a location is unlocked and whether a Day-unlocked prop is on screen stay derived from the day index (D-015/D-017) and must never be written here, since a second copy starts lying the moment the catalog is re-authored. NOTE the single WRITER of this set is deliberately absent: as of this step nothing writes it at all (`MetaSystem` takes the set as a parameter, D-019), so there is no dual-writer risk yet, and squeezing the writer in here would have made Adım 5's `ProfileSaver` work half-done. NOTE also the set needs less machinery than `Wallet` when it does arrive: meta purchases happen on the main screen, outside a day attempt, so there is no `RevertToDayStart` counterpart — the set only ever grows. NOTE finally the real risk this step carried was PLAYER DATA, not code: a botched version bump makes an existing v3 file unreadable and costs a real player their wallet, day and lives; the range check in `IsVersionReadable` (1..4, so v3 still loads), `UpgradeToCurrent` touching only fields a version ADDED, and an explicit "a v3 file keeps every field and owns nothing" test are the three things holding that shut — affects: PlayerProfile.cs, PlayerProfileStore.cs, PlayerProfileStoreTests.cs, ExpoTheExplorer/CLAUDE.md, fingerprint.md, codemap-core.md, meta-plan.md — 2026-08-20

D-021: `GameManager.Awake`'s session half becomes a shared `GameSession` in its own assembly, and `GameSession.Save` becomes the single composer of what reaches the save file -- which cancels the separate `ProfileSaver` class D-015 had planned. Mechanically: a new `ExpoTheExplorer.Session` asmdef (referencing Core, Data, ProgressionSystem, LivesSystem, DaySystem) holding `GameSession`, which builds `GameState`, `Wallet` + `ApplyPersistedBalances`, `LivesManager` + `ApplyPersistedLives`, the parsed Day catalog and the clamped day index, and owns the live `OwnedMetaItemIds` set; `GameManager` constructs it in one line, forwards `State` and `LivesManager` as properties under their ORIGINAL names, drops its `wallet`/`profileStore`/`dayCatalog` fields and its own `ResolveStartingDayIndex`, and its `SaveProfile()` becomes `Session.Save()`; `GameSessionTests` covers 12 cases; the EditMode test asmdef gains the reference — rationale: `meta-plan.md` Adım 5, split into 5a (this) and 5b (the HUD binding and the main screen's root) because the two halves touch different K1 surfaces and a Play-mode failure in one should not be ambiguous with the other. **The extraction exists to stop a duplicate, not to tidy up.** The main screen needs the same five construction steps, and those steps carry four ordering rules -- the wallet before `ApplyPersistedBalances` (GameState's setters are `internal` to ProgressionSystem, so nothing else can seed them), `LivesManager` before anything that can cost a life, the catalog parse before the clamp (which reads `Count`), and `ApplyPersistedBalances` re-taking the day-start snapshot so a session's first retry reverts to the RESTORED balance rather than to zero. A second controller re-implementing those four is precisely the failure the single-writer invariant exists to prevent. **Moving `GameManager` itself was rejected for a concrete reason** (asked directly by the user earlier): `Awake` ends in `ApplyDayStartBoardPreSeed()` + `TicketSlotManager.FillEmptySlots()` and `Update()` runs `TicketSlotManager.Tick`, so the day would RUN behind the shop -- timing tickets out and costing lives -- and a `DontDestroyOnLoad` variant fails differently, since every view in the day scene holds a `[SerializeField] GameManager` that a carried-in object cannot satisfy. Three decisions worth keeping visible. (1) **`ProfileSaver` is cancelled.** `GameSession` already loads the profile and holds the wallet, lives, day index and owned set, so it is what must compose the write; a third class would need a COPY of every field, and a field forgotten in that copy does not throw -- it silently writes a zero over a real player's money. `PlayerProfileStore` stays the file boundary, ProgressionSystem's job per blueprint. This also closes the writer gap D-020 deliberately left open for the owned-meta set. (2) **The folder was forced, not chosen.** `Scripts/Core/` is impossible -- ProgressionSystem, LivesSystem and DaySystem all reference Core already, so a Core-resident GameSession would be a cycle -- and `Scripts/Bootstrap/` carries no asmdef on purpose, since adding one there would pull `GameManager` in and put every hand-wired view reference at risk. Hence `Scripts/Session/`. (3) **`State` and `LivesManager` keep their names as forwarding properties**, which is the single reason roughly a dozen views in `SampleScene` needed no rewiring; renaming them would have turned a refactor into a scene migration. NOTE the coverage this buys: `ResolveStartingDayIndex`'s clamp has guarded a real crash since D-012 -- an index past the last authored Day leaves `CurrentDay` null and throws on the first ticket -- and had NO test, because it lived in a MonoBehaviour in a predefined assembly. It now has four. NOTE also `GameManager` itself remains structurally untestable, so what holds this refactor is `GameSessionTests` plus a Play-mode pass, and the Play-mode pass has NOT been done: the Editor holds the project lock, so verification here is compilation only (Session, Assembly-CSharp and the test assembly, all 0 errors, via temporary csproj files since Unity had not yet generated one for the new asmdef) — affects: ExpoTheExplorer.Session.asmdef (new), GameSession.cs (new), GameManager.cs, GameSessionTests.cs (new), ExpoTheExplorer.Tests.EditMode.asmdef, blueprint.md (two folder-layout lines), codemap-core.md, meta-plan.md — 2026-08-20

D-022: The main screen gets a `GameSession` of its own, and the shared HUD stops depending on the concrete `GameManager`: a new abstract `SessionHost` becomes the thing a scene offers, `GameManager` and a new `MainScreenRoot` both derive from it, and `HudWalletSource` binds to that instead. Mechanically: `SessionHost` (abstract MonoBehaviour in the Session assembly, exposing `GameSession Session`); `GameManager : SessionHost` with `Session` as an `override`; `MainScreenRoot` (Scripts/UI, builds the session in `Awake` from three serialized configs and logs an error if they are missing); `HudWalletSource`'s field becomes `[FormerlySerializedAs("gameManager")] SessionHost sessionHost` with a private `LiveState` helper replacing four repeated null-checks; and `MainScreenSessionSetup`, a menu step that adds the root to the open MainScreen, fills its configs and points the HUD at it — rationale: `meta-plan.md` Adım 5b. A shop cannot spend money on a screen that has no `Wallet`, and the menu HUD reading the save file ONCE (D-013) would show a stale coin count the moment a purchase happened. Two decisions were forced by Unity rather than chosen. (1) **An abstract MonoBehaviour, not the interface the plan called for**: `[SerializeField]` cannot serialize an interface, so an Inspector field that is both draggable and type-checked has to name a concrete type. The alternatives were a `MonoBehaviour` field plus a runtime cast — which makes any component draggable and fails at play time instead of in the Inspector — or a runtime lookup, which the user explicitly ruled out for this binding in D-013. `SessionHost` lives in the Session assembly because it must SEE `GameSession`, and because Assembly-CSharp can derive from an asmdef type while the reverse is impossible. (2) **`[FormerlySerializedAs("gameManager")]` is load-bearing, not tidiness.** `SampleScene`'s HUD prefab instance carries an override under the old field name; renaming without the attribute drops it, and the result is exactly the quiet failure `HudWalletSource` already documents — the HUD stops following the live wallet for a whole day with nothing different on screen. The attribute carries the value across, and it still type-checks because `GameManager` IS a `SessionHost` now. Changing `GameManager`'s base class disturbs nothing serialized: a component's type identity is unchanged by its base. NOTE what deliberately did NOT change: the three HUD views. D-013/D-014's rule is that the mode branch belongs in the SOURCE, never in a view, and they still only read `walletSource`. `MainScreenView` also stayed as it was, so the menu now loads the profile TWICE — once for the session, once for the day number — which is accepted rather than overlooked: converting it means a second scene binding, and the only thing it reads cannot change from a purchase, so a stale read is harmless. Adım 7 rebuilds that screen anyway. NOTE the mode log line's MEANING changed and the comment says so: a missing host used to be expected on the menu, and now means a lost reference in either scene. NOTE finally this step ships with NO new tests, honestly rather than by omission: all three new types are MonoBehaviours and `HudWalletSource` sits in the predefined Assembly-CSharp, which no asmdef test assembly can reference (the wall D-012/D-013 recorded). Verification is compilation only — Session, Assembly-CSharp, Assembly-CSharp-Editor and the test assembly, all 0 errors — and the Play-mode pass is the user's: the first thing to check is `HudWalletSource`'s log line reading "live GameState" in BOTH scenes — affects: SessionHost.cs (new), MainScreenRoot.cs (new), MainScreenSessionSetup.cs (new), GameManager.cs, HudWalletSource.cs, codemap-core.md, codemap-ui.md, codemap-editor.md, meta-plan.md — 2026-08-20

D-023: The meta grounds get a runtime view: `MetaGroundsView` draws one location's background and the props the player has unlocked on it, scrolling vertically when the art is taller than the screen, with a bar to walk between unlocked locations; `MetaGroundsSetup` is a menu step that builds the container and wires it. Mechanically: the background fits by WIDTH with its height derived from the sprite's aspect, sits in a `ScrollRect` with `movementType: Clamped`, and every prop is instantiated at run time as a child of the background with its `normalizedPosition` written straight onto `anchorMin`/`anchorMax`; `MetaResolver.ActiveItems` decides what exists and already returns draw order, so sibling index carries depth; `Refresh()` is public for Adım 8's purchase to call — rationale: `meta-plan.md` Adım 7, with the fit strategy answering open question M10 the way the user chose ("kaydırılabilir olsun"): fitting by height would letterbox every device, while fitting by width leaves vertical scrolling as the only variable, and that variable is ZERO on the phones the art was drawn for (0.463 art against a 0.5625 authored canvas means up to ~18% overflow on a short screen, which is scrolled rather than cropped — cropping would put some props permanently out of reach). Four decisions worth keeping visible. (1) **Props are instantiated, never placed in the scene.** Nineteen hand-placed objects would be a second copy of the catalog, free to disagree with it silently; the catalog stays the single authority (D-015) and the editor step builds only the container. (2) **`Canvas.ForceUpdateCanvases()` before measuring, and it is load-bearing.** Prop sizes derive from the background's WIDTH, and on the frame `Start` runs a stretched rect's width is still zero because Unity has not run its layout pass yet — measuring then sizes every prop to nothing, and the symptom is an empty-looking screen rather than an error. (3) **Props are CHILDREN of the background with the normalized position on their anchors.** This is what K5's "normalize to the background rect, not the screen" was for: they ride the art through every resize and every scroll with no layout code, which is also why scrolling needed no per-prop handling at all. (4) **Stepping is between UNLOCKED locations only** — letting the player walk into a locked one would duplicate in the UI the refusal `MetaPurchase.LocationLocked` already owns; a locked NEXT location shows as an "Unlocks on Day N" hint instead, with the +1 the player-facing day number needs.
