Still no framework, no interface and no state machine: one enum member, one named factory, and the branches that read them. **`kind` went back to being load-bearing.** D-115 had left it as a rejection device — one shape meant nothing to select between — and this restores its original job, because the two shapes share their target and their message and NOTHING else: a forced move's source is a cell, a tray move's is a slot index, and an unset int is 0, which is a real cell AND a real tray. Nothing but the kind can say which pair to trust, which is why both `ResolvedTutorialStep` and `TutorialStep` now carry it and why the unused source is written as **-1 rather than 0** on both sides — a reader that skips the Kind check should get an obviously absent answer, not a plausible one. DaySystem's own `TutorialStepKind` enum is back for the same reason, and **so is GameManager's alias** — with both namespaces declaring the name again, `using ResolvedTutorialStepKind = ...DaySystem.TutorialStepKind` alone would have left every existing bare usage AMBIGUOUS rather than resolved, so the runtime side is aliased to its own name too.

**THE REAL FIND WAS A HOLE, NOT A FEATURE.** `BoardItemDragHandler.OnPointerDown` asked the tutorial about a pickup only when the item was on the BOARD — the comment there was correct that `cellX/cellY` are stale for a seated item, but the conclusion drawn from it was to ask nothing at all. So while ANY step was armed, every tray on screen could be freely emptied, **including during day_00's shipped forced moves**, where the player is supposedly locked to one cell and one tray. `IsTrayPickupAllowed` is the missing twin, defaulting to the strict answer: while a step is armed a tray may be taken from only if that step names it, and only a tray move ever does. That is a live behaviour change to a Day that already ships, and it is the first thing the new tests pin (`DuringAForcedMove_NoTrayCanBeTakenFrom` would have failed before it).

**Where the item comes from is a new authoring block, not a flag on `boardTimeline`.** `trayPreSeed` names a tray, a food and its modifications; `boardTimeline` names a CELL and is played into `BoardGrid`, and folding them together would have given `BoardSpawnEntryJson` a coordinate meaning nothing for half its rows — the same trap `kind` documents. **The seeding is deferred by one tween tick and both reasons are load-bearing**, unlike the spotlight's identical call, which can afford its delay: (1) the opening ticket fill CLEARS every tray via `TrayManager.OnTicketAssigned`, a few statements after `ApplyDayStartBoardPreSeed`, so a synchronous seed would be wiped by design; (2) `BoardView` builds its item containers in its own `Start`, which Unity does not order against `GameManager.Awake`. The consequence reaches the runtime guard: `EnsureCurrentTutorialStepIsPossible` accepts a tray the Day SEEDS as filled even while it is still empty, because a Day whose FIRST step is a tray move would otherwise abort its own tutorial before the item arrived. **The seed goes onto the board and straight back off it**, which looks like a detour and is the opposite — a drag handler is built by `BoardView` for a board cell and by nothing else, and the alternative (writing into `TrayManager` directly) gives a tray that COUNTS an item it does not DRAW, which is the mistake `BoardView.TryGetDragHandler`'s own comment already warns about. Nothing renders between the two halves: `CellChanged` is published synchronously, so the container exists by the time `TryPlaceItem` returns and the seat happens before the method does. `TryAcceptPreSeedDrop` is travel-multiplier **zero** because this item was never moved by anyone — a flight, even a fast one, would say something happened.

**`ValidateDayStartBoard` was WRONG, not merely incomplete, and fixing it was mandatory rather than incidental.** Its premise — "a Day that places items on the Day Start board opens with exactly those items" — stopped being true the moment trays could open with authored items too, and day_03 was reported as unplayable because the cola its first order needs opens in a tray. Seeded items now count toward that check, and **which tray they sit in is deliberately ignored**: the rule asks what the player can get their hands on, and every seated item can be dragged out — which is the very mechanic this Day teaches. The duplicate-source-cell rule became ForcedMove-only for a mirror-image reason: two tray moves share the cell -1/-1 by construction and would have been reported as duplicates of each other. **`DayEditorModel` carries the new block in all four pass-through places plus a fifth the tutorial block never needed** — `ToDayDefinition` resolves it too, because the validator refuses a tray-move step whose source tray nothing fills, so omitting it would make the editor report a correctly authored Day as broken on every Save.

**What did NOT change is worth as much as what did.** `TutorialSpotlightView` needed nothing: `Create` already took a plain `Transform sourceItem` rather than a board cell, so only `WorldTrayView` — which resolves that Transform — had to learn about trays. `SpotlightTraySlotIndex` still answers with the TARGET for both shapes, keeping the "am I the one" check a single comparison, and the new `SpotlightSourceTraySlotIndex` answers the separate question of where the ghost STARTS. The board keeps exactly ONE authored Day-Start entry rather than being emptied as first asked: `ApplyDayStartBoardPreSeed` suppresses the distributor only when a Day authored SOME opening board, so clearing it to zero would have done the opposite of what "nothing should spawn" meant and let `BoardDistributor` fill the board on all three opening assignments. **The one thing left for the author:** the `trays` array on the tray-move TARGET, wired in the scene. Unwired, the step is still fully enforced and only the ghost is missing — the same way a missing source container already fails. affects: `DayJson.cs`, `DayDefinition.cs`, `DayCatalogParser.cs`, `DayValidator.cs`, `TutorialDirector.cs`, `GameManager.cs`, `WorldTrayView.cs`, `BoardItemDragHandler.cs`, `DayEditorModel.cs`, `TutorialDirectorTests.cs`, `day_03.json`.

D-165b: **Two things the compiler could not have told me, both found in one playtest.** The user, 2026-09-03, with a screenshot: "aslında oturttum da bi de içecek olması gerekenden büyük normalde bu kadar büyük olmuyor", plus the ghost's wiring error still firing.

**(1) A zero-duration tween is not an instant one.** `TryAcceptPreSeedDrop` passed `travelMultiplier: 0` to mean "this item is already there", and `SeatInSlot` spends that multiplier on `DOScale`/`DOLocalMove` durations — but DOTween does not apply a zero-duration tween on the frame it is created, so the seeded item simply kept its BOARD scale and board-relative position. **The deeper half was a tween nobody wrote for this path**: a seeded item reaches its tray by being placed on the board and taken straight off it, and that placement makes `BoardView.RefreshCell` treat it as newly appearing, so a POP-IN scale tween toward the item's full board size was already running on that transform — created BEFORE the seat's own, and therefore winning. `BeginFlyInOverride(null)` had suppressed the fly-in but, by the override's own definition, leaves "a plain in-place pop-in". So the fix is `transform.DOKill()` first, then write the destination directly; the tweens created after animate from the final value to itself and cost nothing, which keeps `SeatInSlot`'s contract of returning a live tween for `PlaceInSlotAndDeliver` to hang its `OnComplete` on. **It showed up on the drink because a drink shrinks the most** -- `TrayItemWorldSizeFor` gives it the smallest tray size of the three categories, so board scale reads as most wrong there. `PlaceInSlot`'s haptic is gated on the same multiplier for the same reason: an item nobody dropped must not buzz on the frame the day opens.

**(2) The serialized `trays` array was the wrong shape and is gone.** D-165 shipped it as "one manual step, and it degrades safely", which was true and still wrong: it was a scene edit whose necessity depended on CONTENT -- only the tray that happens to be a tutorial step's TARGET needs it -- so authoring a tray move into a different Day would silently require wiring on a different tray, with nothing but a runtime error to say so. It cost the user a debugging round immediately. The trays now REGISTER THEMSELVES with `GameManager` (`RegisterTray` in `Awake`, read back through `TrayForSlot`), which is not the reference lookup this project refuses -- there is no `Find`, no scene search; each tray hands over its own reference exactly as `TicketCardsView` hands out the cards it spawned. Registering in `Awake` is safe despite that method's own warning about `GameManager` ordering, because the array is a FIELD INITIALIZER and exists from construction. **The feature now needs no scene edit at all**, which is the same place D-163 landed and for the same reason: a slot an author can forget is a bug waiting on the next re-author. affects: `BoardItemDragHandler.cs`, `WorldTrayView.cs`, `GameManager.cs`.

D-165c: **The tray pickup D-165 allowed had nowhere to go back to, and that was a softlock.** The user, 2026-09-03, playtesting: "eğer içeceği alıp trayler dışında bir yere koyarsam da 0,0a götürüyor ve input da kapanıyor hiç bi şey sürükleyemiyorum ama ayarlar açılıyor". Exactly reproduced by reading: `OnEndDrag`'s `else if (!wasOnBoard)` branch scatters a tray pickup onto the board via `board.RequestSpawn`, which lands it in the first free cell — **(0,0)** — and a tray-move step refuses EVERY board cell, so that item could never be picked up again and no tray would accept anything. Settings still opened because it is a UI button and was never gated.

**The guard for this already existed one branch up and this arrived through the door D-165 opened.** The relocation branch carries a comment written for precisely this failure ("the step could be made uncompletable in one gesture... the day was stuck with nothing draggable and no tray accepting") and is gated on `IsBoardRelocationAllowed`. The scatter branch below it was not, and did not need to be: **a tray pickup could not happen during a tutorial at all** until `IsTrayPickupAllowed` started permitting one. Adding a capability re-opened a path whose guard had been reasoned about only for the other origin — worth recording because the bug was not in the new code, it was in old code the new code made reachable. The fix returns the item to the tray it CAME FROM, through that tray's own accept path, which restores `TrayManager`'s count as well as the view (`OnBeginDrag` had already removed it). Only while a step is armed: outside a tutorial, scattering to the board stays the designed behaviour.

**Two view bugs in the same pass, both from the same wrong assumption about the hierarchy.** `FindSeatedItem` walked the TRAY's direct children, but `SeatInSlot` parents an item to `mainDishSlot`/`sideSlot`/`drinkSlot`, one level below — so it found nothing, `RaiseTutorialSpotlight` returned early, and **the tray-to-tray ghost never appeared at all**, which is what the user saw ("içecek hayaleti trayden traye gitmiyor"). `FirstSeatedItem` now walks the same three slots `ReturnItemsToBoard` and `ClearSlot` do. And both ENDS of a tray move are lit now: `litExtras` carried only the target, so lifting the source ITEM out of the dim left it floating over a tray that was still dark — the step says "take it from there", so there has to be a there. affects: `BoardItemDragHandler.cs`, `WorldTrayView.cs`.

D-165d: **A lit tray swallows the press meant for the item inside it, and the reason is invisible from either object.** The user, 2026-09-03, after the ghost was fixed: "seçemiyorum içeceği" -- and then the correction that mattered, "yoo normalde trayden item alabiliyordum ben", which ruled out the pre-existing-bug reading I had reached and pointed at the tutorial itself.

**Four rounds of reading the code found nothing, and a measurement found it in one.** Every term that decides a pickup was verified healthy first (tray index 1, collider enabled, a 0.5x0.5 world hitbox, the right parent), which is what made the eventual answer legible: `Physics2DRaycaster` takes its sort key from the `SpriteRenderer` **on the collider's own GameObject**. A tray root has one. A board item's container does NOT -- its sprites live one level down, as children -- so the raycaster scores any item at a flat **0**, whatever its layers are authored at. The press log said it outright: `TrayArea (1) sortingOrder=598` ahead of `Item_0_0 sortingOrder=0`, same distance, both hit.

**That is why it works in normal play and only breaks under a tutorial.** The tray's sprite is authored at -2, so 0 beats it and the item wins the press. `TutorialDim.LiftSprites` then adds `LitSortingBoost` (600) to every renderer under a lit target, which moves the tray to 598 and leaves the item's container at 0 -- the lift raises the item's own child sprites too, but the raycaster never looks at those. The dim has always done this; D-165 is simply the first feature that ever required pressing an item inside a lit tray, so nothing had reported it. Day 0's tutorial lit tray 0 for the same 600 and nobody noticed, because its steps only ever ask for board items.

**The fix is the source tray standing aside rather than a sorting fight.** While a tray-move step is armed, that step's SOURCE tray disables its own drop collider, so the press lands on the item. It costs nothing to give up: a tray's collider exists to be a DROP target, and that same step's drop gate already refuses this tray -- the item is on its way to the other one. Deliberately NOT fixed by raising the item's sorting order, which would put the art in front of the tray's own frame and change how a full tray looks, and not by moving the item in z, which a sort-order comparison ignores anyway. It is refreshed on every step boundary, and BEFORE `IsTutorialTarget`'s early return -- the tray this concerns is the source, which is by definition not the target, so anything after that return would never run on it.

**Two diagnostics were added and removed to get here, and the second one is the lesson.** The first (`UnityEngine.Input` polling) did not compile in Unity at all: this project runs `activeInputHandler: 1`, the new Input System only, where that class is disabled -- and `dotnet build` cannot see it, so a clean command-line build reported success while the Editor refused the assembly and kept running the previous one. That is a third way the build-without-Unity check can lie, alongside the two already recorded. The replacement asked the EventSystem directly, through an interface the tray was free to implement, and needed no input API. affects: `WorldTrayView.cs`.

D-165e: **A tray move dims no ticket card at all; a forced move still dims every card but its target's.** The user, 2026-09-03, twice -- first "traylerin üstündeki biletler de gözükmeli onlar da karartının arkasında kalıyorlar", then, with a screenshot showing the third card still dark, "hala karartılı ama ya 2. adımda da aydınlık olmalı".

**The two step shapes teach opposite things, so they cannot share a dim rule.** A forced move points at ONE order and the dim's whole job is to say which one -- every other card is noise, and that is D-082's original reasoning, unchanged. A tray move says the opposite: an item can LEAVE one order and go to another. Reading that requires reading the orders, so curtaining the uninvolved card hides half the reason the move makes sense. The first attempt at this only skipped the source card, on a "a lit tray keeps its card lit" pairing rule; the screenshot showed why that was the wrong principle -- it fixed the inconsistency and still left the player unable to see the third order while being taught that orders can trade items.

**Board steps are untouched by construction**, not by a special case: `SpotlightSourceTraySlotIndex` answers -1 for them, and the whole dimming loop now sits behind that being negative, so day_00 curtains exactly the two cards it always did. Lighting a card still costs nothing -- Canvas UI composites above the world-space dim, so bright is the default and only the curtained ones change. **What is deliberately left dim in a tray move: the uninvolved TRAY itself, and the board.** The board being dark is the point (a tray move refuses every cell), and a third tray with no part in the move is not something the player needs to look at -- only its ORDER is. affects: `WorldTrayView.cs`.

D-165f: **A ticket card is not lit by being left alone.** The user, 2026-09-03, with a screenshot after D-165e shipped: "yok olmamış ilk iki bilet siyah hala". D-165e had stopped a tray move from curtaining any card and that changed nothing on screen, which is the whole lesson here.

**The fact neither file stated, and both depended on:** the ticket cards ride `InGameCanvas`, which is Screen Space - **CAMERA** at sortingOrder -1, not Overlay. The dim sheet is a `SpriteRenderer` at `DimSortingOrder` (500), so it covers those cards exactly like anything else the camera renders. `Curtain` is therefore a SECOND layer of dark over a card that is ALREADY behind the sheet -- and removing it leaves the card just as unreadable. The only thing that makes a card legible is `LiftElement`, which is why the target card has always been readable: `Build` lifts it explicitly. `WorldTrayView` carried the opposite belief in a comment ("Canvas UI already composites above the world-space dim, so staying bright is the default"), true for an Overlay canvas and false for this one, and that comment is what made D-165e look sufficient.

**Measured rather than argued, again.** Two rounds of screenshots could not settle it because a Patient and a Normal ticket are different colours before any dim touches them -- day_03 opens Patient/Patient/Normal, which looks like exactly the two-dark-one-light pattern under discussion. A one-line log of what the spotlight actually curtains ended it: step 1 reported two curtained cards, step 2 reported `count=0` while the screen stayed dark, which rules out the curtains and points at the sheet.

**The fix is a `litCards` list passed to `Create`**, lifted beside the target card. Not a second copy of the lift rule inside `WorldTrayView`: the `TutorialDim` instance is private to the spotlight view, and duplicating `LiftElement` would duplicate its restore as well -- the one thing `TutorialDim`'s own note asks callers not to do. The list is empty for a forced move, so day_00 is byte-identical in behaviour. `LiftElement` no-ops on an object that already has a Canvas, so the target card appearing in both places is harmless. affects: `TutorialSpotlightView.cs`, `WorldTrayView.cs`.

D-165g: **The lit card went dark on every step but the first, and the cause was a deferred Destroy racing the next step's lift.** The user, 2026-09-03: "1. ve 2. bilet aydınlık olmalı 3. değil şu an 2. ve 3. aydınlık", then "0 1 aydınlık 2 karanlık olmalı". The symptom named the bug precisely: the cards that WERE lit were exactly the ones passed in `litCards`, and the one that was dark was the TARGET card -- the one `Build` has always lifted on its own. So the list worked and the long-standing lift did not.

**`TutorialDim.Restore` removes a lifted Canvas with `Object.Destroy`, which Unity defers to the end of the frame**, while `WorldTrayView` raised the next step's spotlight in the SAME frame (`DOVirtual.DelayedCall(0f)` can land in the frame it is scheduled from -- DOTween's update and the EventSystem's both run in Update, and their order is fixed for a build, which is why this reproduced every single time). `TutorialDim.Lift` treats "already has a Canvas" as "already lifted" and returns; the stale Canvas is then destroyed a moment later, leaving the card with none and dropping it behind the dim sheet. First step lit (no prior Canvas), every step after it dark. **The class's own note predicted this shape** -- it explains that the restore "has to be synchronous at the boundary, since Unity defers Destroy to the end of the frame while the next step's visuals are built immediately" -- and made the sorting-order half synchronous, while the Canvas half stayed deferred.

**Fixed at the call site rather than in the dim**, and deliberately: the raise is what has an ordering requirement ("after the previous step is torn down"), and a coroutine's `yield return null` is the one construct that states "next frame" without depending on anyone's update order. Changing `Restore` to `DestroyImmediate` would have been the other candidate and was rejected -- it runs from `OnDestroy` as well, where immediate component destruction is the kind of thing Unity intermittently refuses. `SetLink(gameObject)` is not lost: Unity stops a coroutine when its object dies, which is what that call was buying.

**The lighting rule also settled here, one notch back from D-165f.** A tray move lights the two orders it TOUCHES -- target and source -- and dims the third; D-165f had lit all three, on the reasoning that the step is about orders trading items, but a third order is not part of that sentence and lighting it just spends the dim's only signal. affects: `WorldTrayView.cs`.

D-166: **A meta prop gains an authored `scale`, and it is a multiplier read in one function.** The user, 2026-09-04: "meta editörde itemleri scale edebileceğim bi özellik ekler misin". D-015 had deliberately left no such field, and its reasoning was never that a size is unauthorable -- it was that a size authored *instead of* the derivation would be a second authority arguing with the art. The reasoning survives because the field is a MULTIPLIER on the derived size: pixel size x the background's fit scale is still what decides how big a prop is, so re-exporting a location's background at another resolution still moves nothing, and a prop nobody has touched still draws exactly as before (`1`).

**One number, not a per-axis pair.** A prop is a picture of a thing; squashing it on one axis is a mistake the field should not be able to express. `FoodItemConfig.overallScale`, the precedent D-015 named for what this would look like, is the same shape.

**It is read in `MetaLayout.PropSize` and nowhere else**, which is the whole reason it was safe to add. Three surfaces draw the same prop -- the meta screen (`MetaGroundsView`, ghosts and silhouettes included), the day scene's blurred backdrop (`MetaBackdropView`), and the Meta Editor's canvas -- and MetaLayout's own comment already warned that a per-prop scale would make "the same prop two different sizes on the two screens the moment somebody set it in one place". That warning was about a field each caller multiplies in. A field the shared function multiplies in has no second place to set it. **The editor canvas had to be brought INTO that function first**: `MetaLocationLayoutGUI` was keeping its own copy of the formula, so the change added `ExpoTheExplorer.Systems.MetaSystem` to the Editor asmdef and deleted the copy -- a net removal of duplication, and an editor-only arrow that changes nothing about what a build contains.

**Authored two ways, for the reason the position already is.** A grip at the selected prop's top-right corner on the layout canvas, and a numeric field in the prop panel. A size is judged against the art around it, so roughing one out belongs to the eye; matching two props exactly belongs to a number. The grip resizes by ratio from the prop's ANCHOR -- the contact point is the one thing that must not move while a prop grows, which is also why the grip sits opposite it rather than on the bottom edge. Its press deliberately does not also start a position drag (`ScalingIndex` beside `DraggingIndex`, `BeginScaleAt` returning early from `SelectAt`).

**The `Scale` accessor guards `<= 0` back to 1.** The D-075 stale-asset trap is benign for a float -- an absent key deserializes at the initializer -- but a hand-edited or force-zeroed 0 would shrink a prop to nothing, and an invisible prop reads as missing art or a broken resolver, with nothing on screen to point at the real cause. affects: `MetaCatalog.cs`, `MetaLayout.cs`, `MetaLocationLayoutGUI.cs`, `MetaEditorWindow.cs`, `ExpoTheExplorer.Editor.asmdef`, `MetaLayoutTests.cs`.

D-167: **A tray wears the layout its ticket asks for, and the tray OBJECT is never swapped to get it.** The user, 2026-09-04: "trayArea'yı 3 itemlik prefab yapıp 2 tane de varyantını yaptım tek item için ve 2 item için ... bilet 2 item istiyorsa TrayArea2Item bilet 1 item istiyorsa TrayArea1Item 3 item istiyorsa şu anki prefabı kullanmalı". A one-item order used to stand in the three-slot tray with one filled place and two empty holes; `TrayArea1Item` / `TrayArea2Item` are the variants that answer that, and D-129 had already established the premise -- a tray belongs to a TICKET, not to the scene -- so the shape it stands in belongs to the ticket too.

**Instantiating the chosen prefab was the obvious reading of the request and the wrong implementation.** The scene's three trays hold references that only exist in a scene (`gameManager`, `boardView`, `ticketCardsView`) plus a per-instance `slotIndex`, they self-register through `GameManager.RegisterTray` in `Awake` (D-165b made the trays the registrants precisely so no array has to know about them), and `AutoCollectRunner`, `TutorialDirector` and `BoardItemDragHandler` all hold live references to the instance. Destroying and rebuilding that object on every ticket means re-establishing all of it inside the synchronous delivery cascade -- on the file that already carries the project's longest list of ordering hazards. So nothing is instantiated: the tray keeps its identity and ADOPTS the prefab's layout.

**Which is not a compromise, because the layout is the only thing the three prefabs differ in.** `TrayArea2Item` deletes `DrinkSlot` and re-centres `SideSlot` on y; `TrayArea1Item` deletes both and re-centres `MainDishSlot` on x; sprite, collider, rest scale and rest position are identical in all three. A one-item order therefore gets a full-size tray with one centred place, which is what the variants author. If a variant is ever given its own sprite or collider, this stops being equivalent and the copy has to widen with it.

**The layout is READ OFF the prefab's own `WorldTrayView`, not looked up by name.** The fields are typed `WorldTrayView` rather than `GameObject`, so `mainDishSlot`/`sideSlot`/`drinkSlot` are already the authored answer to "does this size have a Side slot" -- the variants null the slots they delete. Presence and position are copied together, since in these prefabs they are one authoring act. No number and no layout rule enters code; the prefab stays the single writer of where a tray's places are (D-140's rule, unchanged).

**`SlotFor` now answers null for a slot that is switched off**, and that one line is what makes the smaller layouts safe without touching `OverflowOrder`: `ResolvePlacementCategory` already skipped a null slot, so an item can never be seated into a place nobody can see. A 2-item ticket authored as Main+Drink (day_03 does exactly this) therefore puts the drink in the 2-item layout's Side slot through the existing overflow chain -- right position, drawn at the side's authored size rather than the drink's, because D-140 deliberately made the destination slot decide both where an item goes and how big it is.

**Applied at two moments, one method, reference-compared against the last ticket.** `PlayTrayEntrance` covers a delivery's next ticket, a retry and a new Day; a poll in `Update` covers the one case that never plays an entrance -- a TIMEOUT, where `TicketSlotManager` cancels and refills the slot without the tray ever leaving. The poll is guarded on `!deliveryInProgress && !trayAnimating && lastKnownCount == 0`, because switching a slot off while an item is still parented to it would take that item off screen. Unwired layout fields mean a tray keeps its authored slots and behaves exactly as it did before -- a forgotten drag costs a three-slot tray on a one-item order, never a broken one. affects: `WorldTrayView.cs`, `TrayArea1Item.prefab`, `TrayArea2Item.prefab`, `TrayArea3Item.prefab`.
